Hello, Doctor. Thank you so much for taking the time to see me. I, um... well, I hope I'm in the right place.

Well, um... yes, I suppose I have been. I hope that doesn't make me sound terrible or rude to my neighbors. It's just... I do go out of my way to avoid that specific intersection now. I'll take a much longer route just so I don't have to walk past it. 

And I've actually stopped signing up for most of the walking tours, and even the local safety meetings. I feel so guilty about missing them, I really do, but I just... I cannot bear the conversations when they inevitably circle back to what happened there. I don't want to make a scene or make anyone else uncomfortable if I get upset, you know? Does that make sense? I hope it's okay that I've been staying away from those things.

Well, um... yes, I'm afraid I do. I really try not to let my mind dwell on it, I promise I do, because I know I should be focusing on the positive things. But sometimes it just... happens.

Well, um... my sleep is mostly fine, I think. I mean, I do wake up sometimes, but isn't that just what happens as we get older?

I feel so guilty about missing them, I really do, but I just... I cannot bear the conversations when they inevitably circle back to what happened there. I'm not entirely sure I know what you mean by that, Doctor. \n\nI don't think I've ever felt like I wasn't... myself, or anything strange like that. I mean, sometimes I get a little distracted, or maybe a bit overwhelmed when there's too much going on, but I think that's just normal for someone my age, isn't it? ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, um... I wouldn't say I'm exactly on edge, Doctor. I really do try to stay calm and not be a nervous Nellie about things. But, I suppose... well, whenever I am near traffic now, I just feel like I have to pay very close attention. 

I find myself watching every single truck, every curb, every pedestrian... almost like it's my responsibility to catch the danger before it can happen again. 

I know I can't control everything, and I really shouldn't be so silly about it. I just want to make sure everyone is safe, you know? I hope I'm not overreacting by doing that. Is that... is that the sort of thing you mean? I just think it pays to be a little extra careful, but I certainly don't want to sound like I'm letting my nerves get the better of me.

Well, um... I really try not to think about it too much, Doctor. I know it's not helpful to dwell on the past, and I certainly don't want to seem like I'm just wallowing in pity. 

But... well, I suppose it does cross my mind more often than I'd like to admit. I just... I keep telling myself that if I had only picked a different route for our walk, or if I had just pushed harder about waiting at that crossing... maybe she might still be here. 

And then, oh dear, I shouldn't say this, but I get so terribly furious all over again at the driver, and even the town for letting that intersection be so dangerous. I know I shouldn't be so angry, and I really try to be forgiving. It's just... it's hard sometimes. 

I hope you don't think poorly of me for having such uncharitable thoughts. I know I should be more accepting of what happened. Is it awful of me to still feel so upset about it?

Oh, um... well, I suppose I did just sort of mention that, didn't I? I hope I'm not repeating myself too much, Doctor, I certainly don't want to waste your time. 

But to answer your question about how often... I suppose it's almost every single day, if I'm being completely honest. I really do try to busy myself with chores so I don't dwell on it, because I know it doesn't do anyone any good. But the thoughts just sneak up on me. 

I just keep replaying it, telling myself that if I had only chosen a different street for our walk, or if I had just grabbed her arm and made us wait at that crossing... she'd still be alive. And then... well, like I said, I get so horribly angry at that driver all over again, and at the town for ignoring how dangerous that intersection is. 

I know it's entirely unhelpful to keep pointing fingers, especially at myself, and I really am trying to be better about it. I know I shouldn't let my mind go there so often and get so worked up. Am I making sense? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.

Is it... is it silly of me to still be so affected by it after two years?\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding people, places, or activities that remind you of your friend or the incident?\n\nPatient: Well, um... yes, I suppose I have been. I hope that doesn't make me sound terrible or rude to my neighbors. It's just... I do go out of my way to avoid that specific intersection now. I'll take a much longer route just so I don't have to walk past it. \n\nAnd I've actually stopped signing up for most of the walking tours, and even the local safety meetings. I feel so guilty about missing them, I really do, but I just... I cannot bear the conversations when they inevitably circle back to what happened there. I don't want to make a scene or make anyone else uncomfortable if I get upset, you know? Does that make sense? I hope it's okay that I've been staying away from those things.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any moments where you felt disconnected from yourself or the world around you since your friend's death?\n\nPatient: Well, um... disconnected? I'm not entirely sure I know what you mean by that, Doctor. \n\nI don't think I've ever felt like I wasn't... myself, or anything strange like that. I mean, sometimes I get a little distracted, or maybe a bit overwhelmed when there's too much going on, but I think that's just normal for someone my age, isn't it? I certainly haven't lost touch with reality or anything. \n\nI try very hard to stay present and keep up with everything. Is it... is it bad if I don't quite understand what you're looking for? I want to make sure I'm answering your questions properly. I hope I'm not being difficult.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any recurring thoughts or memories about your friend's death that you can't seem to get out of your head?\n\nPatient: Well, um... yes, I'm afraid I do. I really try not to let my mind dwell on it, I promise I do, because I know I should be focusing on the positive things. But sometimes it just... happens. \n\nIt doesn't even take much. Just hearing the chirp of a crosswalk signal, or... or the loud growl of a big truck going by. Even just seeing one of those walking-tour maps we used to use. It's like my brain just snaps right back to that day, and it's so sharp, it's almost like I'm right there again. I can see her stepping off the curb, and I can't stop it from playing out in my head. \n\nI'm so sorry, I know I shouldn't let these little things bother me so much. I feel like I ought to have better control over my own thoughts by now. Is it... is it terribly weak of me to still get so rattled by a crosswalk signal? I hope you don't think I'm just feeling sorry for myself.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble sleeping at night?\n\nPatient: Well, um... my sleep is mostly fine, I think. I mean, I do wake up sometimes, but isn't that just what happens as we get older? \n\nI really try to make sure I get enough rest so I'm not a bother to anyone the next day. Sometimes it takes a little while to drift off, maybe because I'm just thinking about what I need to do around the house, but I wouldn't call it trouble sleeping. I certainly don't want to complain about something as silly as tossing and turning a bit. \n\nI hope that's the kind of answer you were looking for? I just don't want to make a fuss over a few restless nights if it's perfectly normal for someone my age.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel on edge or easily startled?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I wouldn't say I'm exactly on edge, Doctor. I really do try to stay calm and not be a nervous Nellie about things. But, I suppose... well, whenever I am near traffic now, I just feel like I have to pay very close attention. \n\nI find myself watching every single truck, every curb, every pedestrian... almost like it's my responsibility to catch the danger before it can happen again. \n\nI know I can't control everything, and I really shouldn't be so silly about it. I just want to make sure everyone is safe, you know? I hope I'm not overreacting by doing that. Is that... is that the sort of thing you mean? I just think it pays to be a little extra careful, but I certainly don't want to sound like I'm letting my nerves get the better of me.\n\nDoctor: How often do you think about the guilt you feel related to your friend's death?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I really try not to think about it too much, Doctor. I know it's not helpful to dwell on the past, and I certainly don't want to seem like I'm just wallowing in pity. \n\nBut... well, I suppose it does cross my mind more often than I'd like to admit. I just... I keep telling myself that if I had only picked a different route for our walk, or if I had just pushed harder about waiting at that crossing... maybe she might still be here. \n\nAnd then, oh dear, I shouldn't say this, but I get so terribly furious all over again at the driver, and even the town for letting that intersection be so dangerous. I know I shouldn't be so angry, and I really try to be forgiving. It's just... it's hard sometimes. \n\nI hope you don't think poorly of me for having such uncharitable thoughts. I know I should be more accepting of what happened. Is it awful of me to still feel so upset about it?\n\nDoctor: How often would you say you think about what you could have done differently to prevent your friend's death?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I suppose I did just sort of mention that, didn't I? I hope I'm not repeating myself too much, Doctor, I certainly don't want to waste your time. \n\nBut to answer your question about how often... I suppose it's almost every single day, if I'm being completely honest. I really do try to busy myself with chores so I don't dwell on it, because I know it doesn't do anyone any good. But the thoughts just sneak up on me. \n\nI just keep replaying it, telling myself that if I had only chosen a different street for our walk, or if I had just grabbed her arm and made us wait at that crossing... she'd still be alive. And then... well, like I said, I get so horribly angry at that driver all over again, and at the town for ignoring how dangerous that intersection is. \n\nI know it's entirely unhelpful to keep pointing fingers, especially at myself, and I really am trying to be better about it. I know I shouldn't let my mind go there so often and get so worked up. Am I making sense? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Well, um... I really do try to keep up with my usual routine, Doctor. I don't want to be a bother to anyone or seem like I'm hiding away. But, I suppose... well, I do go quite a bit out of my way to avoid that particular intersection now. I just find it easier to take a different route, even if it's longer.

I know I should stay involved, and I feel terribly guilty for stepping back. It's just... I really can't bear it when the conversations circle back to what happened there. It just brings up all those unpleasant feelings again, and I don't want to make anyone uncomfortable by getting upset. 

I hope you don't think I'm just being avoidant or cowardly. I really am trying my best to stay active. Is it alright that I've been skipping those things? I just thought it might be better for everyone if I stayed away for a little while until I can be more pleasant company.

Oh, a number... um, well, I don't want to sound dramatic, Doctor. I know everyone expects me to be moving on by now, and I really, really try to put on a brave face so I don't burden anyone.

It hasn't eased up at all, not the way people told me it would. It's just this mix of awful feelings I've been living with every single day since she was killed. 

So, I suppose... maybe an 8? Or a 9? I hope that doesn't sound too extreme. I just... I really thought I'd be doing better by now. Is it normal to still feel it that strongly after all this time? I just want to make sure I'm doing the right things to get better.

It's just a lot of guilt, and fear, and... and anger, which really isn't like me at all, I promise. It just hasn't eased up the way everyone seemed to think it would by now. \n\nI've been trying my best to handle it on my own, I really have, but... is it okay that I'm coming in for this? I just didn't know what else to do, and I hope I'm not taking up time from someone who needs it more.\n\nDoctor: How long has it been since your friend was killed?\n\nPatient: Well, um... it happened while we were on a walking tour together. And, oh, I suppose it's been about two years and three months ago now. \n\nI know that probably sounds like a terribly long time to still be... well, to still be struggling with it, doesn't it? I really thought I would have moved past it by now, and I'm so sorry if I'm overreacting by coming in after all this time. Is it... is it silly of me to still be so affected by it after two years?\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding people, places, or activities that remind you of your friend or the incident?\n\nPatient: Well, um... yes, I suppose I have been. I hope that doesn't make me sound terrible or rude to my neighbors. It's just... I do go out of my way to avoid that specific intersection now. I'll take a much longer route just so I don't have to walk past it. \n\nAnd I've actually stopped signing up for most of the walking tours, and even the local safety meetings. I feel so guilty about missing them, I really do, but I just... I cannot bear the conversations when they inevitably circle back to what happened there. I don't want to make a scene or make anyone else uncomfortable if I get upset, you know? Does that make sense? I hope it's okay that I've been staying away from those things.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any moments where you felt disconnected from yourself or the world around you since your friend's death?\n\nPatient: Well, um... disconnected? I'm not entirely sure I know what you mean by that, Doctor. \n\nI don't think I've ever felt like I wasn't... myself, or anything strange like that. I mean, sometimes I get a little distracted, or maybe a bit overwhelmed when there's too much going on, but I think that's just normal for someone my age, isn't it? I certainly haven't lost touch with reality or anything. \n\nI try very hard to stay present and keep up with everything. Is it... is it bad if I don't quite understand what you're looking for? I want to make sure I'm answering your questions properly. I hope I'm not being difficult.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any recurring thoughts or memories about your friend's death that you can't seem to get out of your head?\n\nPatient: Well, um... yes, I'm afraid I do. I really try not to let my mind dwell on it, I promise I do, because I know I should be focusing on the positive things. But sometimes it just... happens. \n\nIt doesn't even take much. Just hearing the chirp of a crosswalk signal, or... or the loud growl of a big truck going by. Even just seeing one of those walking-tour maps we used to use. It's like my brain just snaps right back to that day, and it's so sharp, it's almost like I'm right there again. I can see her stepping off the curb, and I can't stop it from playing out in my head. \n\nI'm so sorry, I know I shouldn't let these little things bother me so much. I feel like I ought to have better control over my own thoughts by now. Is it... is it terribly weak of me to still get so rattled by a crosswalk signal? I hope you don't think I'm just feeling sorry for myself.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble sleeping at night?\n\nPatient: Well, um... my sleep is mostly fine, I think. I mean, I do wake up sometimes, but isn't that just what happens as we get older? \n\nI really try to make sure I get enough rest so I'm not a bother to anyone the next day. Sometimes it takes a little while to drift off, maybe because I'm just thinking about what I need to do around the house, but I wouldn't call it trouble sleeping. I certainly don't want to complain about something as silly as tossing and turning a bit. \n\nI hope that's the kind of answer you were looking for? I just don't want to make a fuss over a few restless nights if it's perfectly normal for someone my age.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel on edge or easily startled?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I wouldn't say I'm exactly on edge, Doctor. I really do try to stay calm and not be a nervous Nellie about things. But, I suppose... well, whenever I am near traffic now, I just feel like I have to pay very close attention. \n\nI find myself watching every single truck, every curb, every pedestrian... almost like it's my responsibility to catch the danger before it can happen again. \n\nI know I can't control everything, and I really shouldn't be so silly about it. I just want to make sure everyone is safe, you know? I hope I'm not overreacting by doing that. Is that... is that the sort of thing you mean? I just think it pays to be a little extra careful, but I certainly don't want to sound like I'm letting my nerves get the better of me.\n\nDoctor: How often do you think about the guilt you feel related to your friend's death?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I really try not to think about it too much, Doctor. I know it's not helpful to dwell on the past, and I certainly don't want to seem like I'm just wallowing in pity. \n\nBut... well, I suppose it does cross my mind more often than I'd like to admit. I just... I keep telling myself that if I had only picked a different route for our walk, or if I had just pushed harder about waiting at that crossing... maybe she might still be here. \n\nAnd then, oh dear, I shouldn't say this, but I get so terribly furious all over again at the driver, and even the town for letting that intersection be so dangerous. I know I shouldn't be so angry, and I really try to be forgiving. It's just... it's hard sometimes. \n\nI hope you don't think poorly of me for having such uncharitable thoughts. I know I should be more accepting of what happened. Is it awful of me to still feel so upset about it?\n\nDoctor: How often would you say you think about what you could have done differently to prevent your friend's death?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I suppose I did just sort of mention that, didn't I? I hope I'm not repeating myself too much, Doctor, I certainly don't want to waste your time. \n\nBut to answer your question about how often... I suppose it's almost every single day, if I'm being completely honest. I really do try to busy myself with chores so I don't dwell on it, because I know it doesn't do anyone any good. But the thoughts just sneak up on me. \n\nI just keep replaying it, telling myself that if I had only chosen a different street for our walk, or if I had just grabbed her arm and made us wait at that crossing... she'd still be alive. And then... well, like I said, I get so horribly angry at that driver all over again, and at the town for ignoring how dangerous that intersection is. \n\nI know it's entirely unhelpful to keep pointing fingers, especially at myself, and I really am trying to be better about it. I know I shouldn't let my mind go there so often and get so worked up. Am I making sense? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Have you avoided any other activities or places beyond the intersection and walking tours since your friend's death?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I really do try to keep up with my usual routine, Doctor. I don't want to be a bother to anyone or seem like I'm hiding away. But, I suppose... well, I do go quite a bit out of my way to avoid that particular intersection now. I just find it easier to take a different route, even if it's longer. \n\nAnd, um... I actually haven't been signing up for most of the walking tours lately, or even the neighborhood safety meetings. I know I should stay involved, and I feel terribly guilty for stepping back. It's just... I really can't bear it when the conversations circle back to what happened there. It just brings up all those unpleasant feelings again, and I don't want to make anyone uncomfortable by getting upset. \n\nI hope you don't think I'm just being avoidant or cowardly. I really am trying my best to stay active. Is it alright that I've been skipping those things? I just thought it might be better for everyone if I stayed away for a little while until I can be more pleasant company.\n\nDoctor: How would you rate the intensity of the guilt you feel about your friend's death, on a scale from 1 to 10?\n\nPatient: Oh, a number... um, well, I don't want to sound dramatic, Doctor. I know everyone expects me to be moving on by now, and I really, really try to put on a brave face so I don't burden anyone. \n\nBut if I'm being perfectly honest with you... the guilt, along with just this constant fear and... and that anger I mentioned... it's just always there. It hasn't eased up at all, not the way people told me it would. It's just this mix of awful feelings I've been living with every single day since she was killed. \n\nSo, I suppose... maybe an 8? Or a 9? I hope that doesn't sound too extreme. I just... I really thought I'd be doing better by now. Is it normal to still feel it that strongly after all this time? I just want to make sure I'm doing the right things to get better.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Your goal is to answer the doctor's question naturally, using the\ninformation you are given.\n\nAnswer with the [Targeted Info], told in the way [Conversation Style] describes.\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: How often would you say you think about what you could have done differently to prevent your friend's death?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I suppose I did just sort of mention that, didn't I? I hope I'm not repeating myself too much, Doctor, I certainly don't want to waste your time. \n\nBut to answer your question about how often... I suppose it's almost every single day, if I'm being completely honest. I really do try to busy myself with chores so I don't dwell on it, because I know it doesn't do anyone any good. But the thoughts just sneak up on me. \n\nI just keep replaying it, telling myself that if I had only chosen a different street for our walk, or if I had just grabbed her arm and made us wait at that crossing... she'd still be alive. And then... well, like I said, I get so horribly angry at that driver all over again, and at the town for ignoring how dangerous that intersection is. \n\nI know it's entirely unhelpful to keep pointing fingers, especially at myself, and I really am trying to be better about it. I know I shouldn't let my mind go there so often and get so worked up. Am I making sense? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Have you avoided any other activities or places beyond the intersection and walking tours since your friend's death?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I really do try to keep up with my usual routine, Doctor. I don't want to be a bother to anyone or seem like I'm hiding away. But, I suppose... well, I do go quite a bit out of my way to avoid that particular intersection now. I just find it easier to take a different route, even if it's longer. \n\nAnd, um... I actually haven't been signing up for most of the walking tours lately, or even the neighborhood safety meetings. I know I should stay involved, and I feel terribly guilty for stepping back. It's just... I really can't bear it when the conversations circle back to what happened there. It just brings up all those unpleasant feelings again, and I don't want to make anyone uncomfortable by getting upset. \n\nI hope you don't think I'm just being avoidant or cowardly. I really am trying my best to stay active. Is it alright that I've been skipping those things? I just thought it might be better for everyone if I stayed away for a little while until I can be more pleasant company.\n\nDoctor: How would you rate the intensity of the guilt you feel about your friend's death, on a scale from 1 to 10?\n\nPatient: Oh, a number... um, well, I don't want to sound dramatic, Doctor. I know everyone expects me to be moving on by now, and I really, really try to put on a brave face so I don't burden anyone. \n\nBut if I'm being perfectly honest with you... the guilt, along with just this constant fear and... and that anger I mentioned... it's just always there. It hasn't eased up at all, not the way people told me it would. It's just this mix of awful feelings I've been living with every single day since she was killed. \n\nSo, I suppose... maybe an 8? People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, yes... I suppose I do. I know I touched on this just a few moments ago, and I'm so sorry for repeating myself, Doctor. I really don't want to sound like a broken record or waste our time together... but yes. 

I just keep telling myself that if I had only picked a different route for us, or if I had just pushed harder about waiting at that crossing, she might still be alive today. It's a dreadful thing to carry around, and I feel so ashamed that I can't just let it go. And then... well, then I get furious all over again at the driver, and at the town for letting that intersection stay so dangerous and letting this happen. 

I know it’s terribly ungracious of me to hold onto so much blame and anger, and I really do try to stop myself when I start pointing fingers like that. It's just so hard to control when the thoughts pop up. I hope you can understand... does that make sense? I just want to be sure I'm giving you the answers you need.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, um... I really do try to stay connected, Doctor. I make it a point to go to the senior center and sit with the same ladies I've known for years. I don't want them to think I'm ignoring them or being rude. 

But... if I'm being completely honest, and I hope this doesn't make me sound cold or unfeeling... I just don't feel the same way around them anymore. Even when I'm right there at the table, listening to them talk, I feel sort of... separated from it all. It's almost as if they're chatting on one side of a window, and I'm just watching from the other side. 

I feel so terrible about it because they've been so kind to me, and I really should be more engaged. I just can't seem to bridge that gap right now. Is it awful of me to feel so disconnected from my friends? I really am trying to be present, I promise.

I can see her stepping off the curb, and I can't stop it from playing out in my head. \n\nI'm so sorry, I know I shouldn't let these little things bother me so much. I feel like I ought to have better control over my own thoughts by now. Is it... is it terribly weak of me to still get so rattled by a crosswalk signal? I mean, I do wake up sometimes, but isn't that just what happens as we get older? \n\nI really try to make sure I get enough rest so I'm not a bother to anyone the next day. I just want to make sure everyone is safe, you know? I hope I'm not overreacting by doing that. Is that... is that the sort of thing you mean? Is it... is it silly of me to still be so affected by it after two years?\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding people, places, or activities that remind you of your friend or the incident?\n\nPatient: Well, um... yes, I suppose I have been. I hope that doesn't make me sound terrible or rude to my neighbors. It's just... I do go out of my way to avoid that specific intersection now. I'll take a much longer route just so I don't have to walk past it. \n\nAnd I've actually stopped signing up for most of the walking tours, and even the local safety meetings. I feel so guilty about missing them, I really do, but I just... I cannot bear the conversations when they inevitably circle back to what happened there. I don't want to make a scene or make anyone else uncomfortable if I get upset, you know? Does that make sense? I hope it's okay that I've been staying away from those things.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any moments where you felt disconnected from yourself or the world around you since your friend's death?\n\nPatient: Well, um... disconnected? I'm not entirely sure I know what you mean by that, Doctor. \n\nI don't think I've ever felt like I wasn't... myself, or anything strange like that. I mean, sometimes I get a little distracted, or maybe a bit overwhelmed when there's too much going on, but I think that's just normal for someone my age, isn't it? I certainly haven't lost touch with reality or anything. \n\nI try very hard to stay present and keep up with everything. Is it... is it bad if I don't quite understand what you're looking for? I want to make sure I'm answering your questions properly. I hope I'm not being difficult.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any recurring thoughts or memories about your friend's death that you can't seem to get out of your head?\n\nPatient: Well, um... yes, I'm afraid I do. I really try not to let my mind dwell on it, I promise I do, because I know I should be focusing on the positive things. But sometimes it just... happens. \n\nIt doesn't even take much. Just hearing the chirp of a crosswalk signal, or... or the loud growl of a big truck going by. Even just seeing one of those walking-tour maps we used to use. It's like my brain just snaps right back to that day, and it's so sharp, it's almost like I'm right there again. I can see her stepping off the curb, and I can't stop it from playing out in my head. \n\nI'm so sorry, I know I shouldn't let these little things bother me so much. I feel like I ought to have better control over my own thoughts by now. Is it... is it terribly weak of me to still get so rattled by a crosswalk signal? I hope you don't think I'm just feeling sorry for myself.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble sleeping at night?\n\nPatient: Well, um... my sleep is mostly fine, I think. I mean, I do wake up sometimes, but isn't that just what happens as we get older? \n\nI really try to make sure I get enough rest so I'm not a bother to anyone the next day. Sometimes it takes a little while to drift off, maybe because I'm just thinking about what I need to do around the house, but I wouldn't call it trouble sleeping. I certainly don't want to complain about something as silly as tossing and turning a bit. \n\nI hope that's the kind of answer you were looking for? I just don't want to make a fuss over a few restless nights if it's perfectly normal for someone my age.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel on edge or easily startled?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I wouldn't say I'm exactly on edge, Doctor. I really do try to stay calm and not be a nervous Nellie about things. But, I suppose... well, whenever I am near traffic now, I just feel like I have to pay very close attention. \n\nI find myself watching every single truck, every curb, every pedestrian... almost like it's my responsibility to catch the danger before it can happen again. \n\nI know I can't control everything, and I really shouldn't be so silly about it. I just want to make sure everyone is safe, you know? I hope I'm not overreacting by doing that. Is that... is that the sort of thing you mean? I just think it pays to be a little extra careful, but I certainly don't want to sound like I'm letting my nerves get the better of me.\n\nDoctor: How often do you think about the guilt you feel related to your friend's death?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I really try not to think about it too much, Doctor. I know it's not helpful to dwell on the past, and I certainly don't want to seem like I'm just wallowing in pity. \n\nBut... well, I suppose it does cross my mind more often than I'd like to admit. I just... I keep telling myself that if I had only picked a different route for our walk, or if I had just pushed harder about waiting at that crossing... maybe she might still be here. \n\nAnd then, oh dear, I shouldn't say this, but I get so terribly furious all over again at the driver, and even the town for letting that intersection be so dangerous. I know I shouldn't be so angry, and I really try to be forgiving. It's just... it's hard sometimes. \n\nI hope you don't think poorly of me for having such uncharitable thoughts. I know I should be more accepting of what happened. Is it awful of me to still feel so upset about it?\n\nDoctor: How often would you say you think about what you could have done differently to prevent your friend's death?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I suppose I did just sort of mention that, didn't I? I hope I'm not repeating myself too much, Doctor, I certainly don't want to waste your time. \n\nBut to answer your question about how often... I suppose it's almost every single day, if I'm being completely honest. I really do try to busy myself with chores so I don't dwell on it, because I know it doesn't do anyone any good. But the thoughts just sneak up on me. \n\nI just keep replaying it, telling myself that if I had only chosen a different street for our walk, or if I had just grabbed her arm and made us wait at that crossing... she'd still be alive. And then... well, like I said, I get so horribly angry at that driver all over again, and at the town for ignoring how dangerous that intersection is. \n\nI know it's entirely unhelpful to keep pointing fingers, especially at myself, and I really am trying to be better about it. I know I shouldn't let my mind go there so often and get so worked up. Am I making sense? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Have you avoided any other activities or places beyond the intersection and walking tours since your friend's death?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I really do try to keep up with my usual routine, Doctor. I don't want to be a bother to anyone or seem like I'm hiding away. But, I suppose... well, I do go quite a bit out of my way to avoid that particular intersection now. I just find it easier to take a different route, even if it's longer. \n\nAnd, um... I actually haven't been signing up for most of the walking tours lately, or even the neighborhood safety meetings. I know I should stay involved, and I feel terribly guilty for stepping back. It's just... I really can't bear it when the conversations circle back to what happened there. It just brings up all those unpleasant feelings again, and I don't want to make anyone uncomfortable by getting upset. \n\nI hope you don't think I'm just being avoidant or cowardly. I really am trying my best to stay active. Is it alright that I've been skipping those things? I just thought it might be better for everyone if I stayed away for a little while until I can be more pleasant company.\n\nDoctor: How would you rate the intensity of the guilt you feel about your friend's death, on a scale from 1 to 10?\n\nPatient: Oh, a number... um, well, I don't want to sound dramatic, Doctor. I know everyone expects me to be moving on by now, and I really, really try to put on a brave face so I don't burden anyone. \n\nBut if I'm being perfectly honest with you... the guilt, along with just this constant fear and... and that anger I mentioned... it's just always there. It hasn't eased up at all, not the way people told me it would. It's just this mix of awful feelings I've been living with every single day since she was killed. \n\nSo, I suppose... maybe an 8? Or a 9? I hope that doesn't sound too extreme. I just... I really thought I'd be doing better by now. Is it normal to still feel it that strongly after all this time? I just want to make sure I'm doing the right things to get better.\n\nDoctor: Do you blame yourself for your friend's death?\n\nPatient: Oh, yes... I suppose I do. It's a dreadful thing to carry around, and I feel so ashamed that I can't just let it go. And then... well, then I get furious all over again at the driver, and at the town for letting that intersection stay so dangerous and letting this happen. \n\nI know it’s terribly ungracious of me to hold onto so much blame and anger, and I really do try to stop myself when I start pointing fingers like that. Oh, um... a bad person? I... I wouldn't want to use that word exactly, Doctor. I really try to be a good person, I always have. I volunteer, I help out when I can... I don't want you to think I'm some terrible, malicious woman. 

But... well, I know I mentioned this earlier, and I'm sorry to bring it up again, but it's just so hard to escape. I do keep telling myself that if I had just picked a different route for our walk, or if I had pushed harder about waiting at that awful crossing... she might still be here. And when I think about that, I do feel like I failed her. It makes me feel... well, very small and ashamed. 

And then, almost immediately, I just get so furious all over again at the driver, and at the town for letting that intersection stay so dangerous and letting this happen. I know I shouldn't be so angry, and I really try to keep those uncharitable thoughts in check, because I know it doesn't do any good to be so bitter. 

I hope that doesn't make me sound like a bad person to you? I'm really trying to work through these feelings properly, I promise. Am I making sense? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right picture of what's going on in my head.

I hope I'm not being difficult.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any recurring thoughts or memories about your friend's death that you can't seem to get out of your head?\n\nPatient: Well, um... yes, I'm afraid I do. I really try not to let my mind dwell on it, I promise I do, because I know I should be focusing on the positive things. But sometimes it just... happens. \n\nIt doesn't even take much. Just hearing the chirp of a crosswalk signal, or... or the loud growl of a big truck going by. Even just seeing one of those walking-tour maps we used to use. It's like my brain just snaps right back to that day, and it's so sharp, it's almost like I'm right there again. I can see her stepping off the curb, and I can't stop it from playing out in my head. \n\nI'm so sorry, I know I shouldn't let these little things bother me so much. I feel like I ought to have better control over my own thoughts by now. Is it... is it terribly weak of me to still get so rattled by a crosswalk signal? I hope you don't think I'm just feeling sorry for myself.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble sleeping at night?\n\nPatient: Well, um... my sleep is mostly fine, I think. I mean, I do wake up sometimes, but isn't that just what happens as we get older? \n\nI really try to make sure I get enough rest so I'm not a bother to anyone the next day. Sometimes it takes a little while to drift off, maybe because I'm just thinking about what I need to do around the house, but I wouldn't call it trouble sleeping. I certainly don't want to complain about something as silly as tossing and turning a bit. \n\nI hope that's the kind of answer you were looking for? I just don't want to make a fuss over a few restless nights if it's perfectly normal for someone my age.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel on edge or easily startled?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I wouldn't say I'm exactly on edge, Doctor. I really do try to stay calm and not be a nervous Nellie about things. But, I suppose... well, whenever I am near traffic now, I just feel like I have to pay very close attention. \n\nI find myself watching every single truck, every curb, every pedestrian... almost like it's my responsibility to catch the danger before it can happen again. \n\nI know I can't control everything, and I really shouldn't be so silly about it. I just want to make sure everyone is safe, you know? I hope I'm not overreacting by doing that. Is that... is that the sort of thing you mean? I just think it pays to be a little extra careful, but I certainly don't want to sound like I'm letting my nerves get the better of me.\n\nDoctor: How often do you think about the guilt you feel related to your friend's death?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I really try not to think about it too much, Doctor. I know it's not helpful to dwell on the past, and I certainly don't want to seem like I'm just wallowing in pity. \n\nBut... well, I suppose it does cross my mind more often than I'd like to admit. I just... I know I shouldn't be so angry, and I really try to be forgiving. It's just... it's hard sometimes. \n\nI hope you don't think poorly of me for having such uncharitable thoughts. I know I should be more accepting of what happened. Is it awful of me to still feel so upset about it?\n\nDoctor: How often would you say you think about what you could have done differently to prevent your friend's death?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I suppose I did just sort of mention that, didn't I? I suppose it's almost every single day, if I'm being completely honest. I really do try to busy myself with chores so I don't dwell on it, because I know it doesn't do anyone any good. I know I shouldn't let my mind go there so often and get so worked up. Am I making sense? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Have you avoided any other activities or places beyond the intersection and walking tours since your friend's death?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I just thought it might be better for everyone if I stayed away for a little while until I can be more pleasant company.\n\nDoctor: How would you rate the intensity of the guilt you feel about your friend's death, on a scale from 1 to 10?\n\nPatient: Oh, a number... um, well, I don't want to sound dramatic, Doctor. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, um... I really do try to keep busy, Doctor. I don't want to be a bother to anyone or just sit around the house moping, so I still make sure to write everything down on my calendar and keep my schedule. 

But... oh, it's a bit embarrassing to admit, if I'm being perfectly honest. I used to just love planning out my garden beds for the spring, and quilting with the ladies. We'd go to theater outings, and I used to argue so happily at our book club. I was always so involved and energetic about it all. 

Lately, though... when I look at those things on the calendar, I just... I feel almost nothing. It’s the strangest thing. It almost feels like those hobbies belong to someone I used to be, rather than who I am now. 

I feel so terribly guilty saying that out loud, because I know I should be grateful to have such lovely hobbies and things to do. I hope you don't think I'm just giving up or being lazy. I really am trying to push through it. Does that make sense, what I'm trying to say? I just want to be sure I'm giving you the right information.

I just want to make sure everyone is safe, you know? I hope I'm not overreacting by doing that. Is that... is that the sort of thing you mean? I just think it pays to be a little extra careful, but I certainly don't want to sound like I'm letting my nerves get the better of me.\n\nDoctor: How often do you think about the guilt you feel related to your friend's death?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I really try not to think about it too much, Doctor. I know it's not helpful to dwell on the past, and I certainly don't want to seem like I'm just wallowing in pity. \n\nBut... well, I suppose it does cross my mind more often than I'd like to admit. I just... I really am trying my best to stay active. Is it alright that I've been skipping those things? I just thought it might be better for everyone if I stayed away for a little while until I can be more pleasant company.\n\nDoctor: How would you rate the intensity of the guilt you feel about your friend's death, on a scale from 1 to 10?\n\nPatient: Oh, a number... um, well, I don't want to sound dramatic, Doctor. I hope you don't think I'm just feeling sorry for myself.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble sleeping at night?\n\nPatient: Well, um... my sleep is mostly fine, I think. I mean, I do wake up sometimes, but isn't that just what happens as we get older? \n\nI really try to make sure I get enough rest so I'm not a bother to anyone the next day. Sometimes it takes a little while to drift off, maybe because I'm just thinking about what I need to do around the house, but I wouldn't call it trouble sleeping. I certainly don't want to complain about something as silly as tossing and turning a bit. \n\nI hope that's the kind of answer you were looking for? I just don't want to make a fuss over a few restless nights if it's perfectly normal for someone my age.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel on edge or easily startled?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I wouldn't say I'm exactly on edge, Doctor. I really do try to stay calm and not be a nervous Nellie about things. But, I suppose... well, whenever I am near traffic now, I just feel like I have to pay very close attention. \n\nI find myself watching every single truck, every curb, every pedestrian... almost like it's my responsibility to catch the danger before it can happen again. \n\nI know I can't control everything, and I really shouldn't be so silly about it. I just want to make sure everyone is safe, you know? I hope I'm not overreacting by doing that. Is that... is that the sort of thing you mean? I just think it pays to be a little extra careful, but I certainly don't want to sound like I'm letting my nerves get the better of me.\n\nDoctor: How often do you think about the guilt you feel related to your friend's death?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I really try not to think about it too much, Doctor. I know it's not helpful to dwell on the past, and I certainly don't want to seem like I'm just wallowing in pity. \n\nBut... well, I suppose it does cross my mind more often than I'd like to admit. I just... I keep telling myself that if I had only picked a different route for our walk, or if I had just pushed harder about waiting at that crossing... maybe she might still be here. \n\nAnd then, oh dear, I shouldn't say this, but I get so terribly furious all over again at the driver, and even the town for letting that intersection be so dangerous. I know I shouldn't be so angry, and I really try to be forgiving. It's just... it's hard sometimes. \n\nI hope you don't think poorly of me for having such uncharitable thoughts. I know I should be more accepting of what happened. Is it awful of me to still feel so upset about it?\n\nDoctor: How often would you say you think about what you could have done differently to prevent your friend's death?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I suppose I did just sort of mention that, didn't I? I hope I'm not repeating myself too much, Doctor, I certainly don't want to waste your time. \n\nBut to answer your question about how often... I suppose it's almost every single day, if I'm being completely honest. I really do try to busy myself with chores so I don't dwell on it, because I know it doesn't do anyone any good. But the thoughts just sneak up on me. \n\nI just keep replaying it, telling myself that if I had only chosen a different street for our walk, or if I had just grabbed her arm and made us wait at that crossing... she'd still be alive. And then... well, like I said, I get so horribly angry at that driver all over again, and at the town for ignoring how dangerous that intersection is. \n\nI know it's entirely unhelpful to keep pointing fingers, especially at myself, and I really am trying to be better about it. I know I shouldn't let my mind go there so often and get so worked up. Am I making sense? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Have you avoided any other activities or places beyond the intersection and walking tours since your friend's death?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I really do try to keep up with my usual routine, Doctor. I don't want to be a bother to anyone or seem like I'm hiding away. But, I suppose... well, I do go quite a bit out of my way to avoid that particular intersection now. I just find it easier to take a different route, even if it's longer. \n\nAnd, um... I actually haven't been signing up for most of the walking tours lately, or even the neighborhood safety meetings. I know I should stay involved, and I feel terribly guilty for stepping back. It's just... I really can't bear it when the conversations circle back to what happened there. It just brings up all those unpleasant feelings again, and I don't want to make anyone uncomfortable by getting upset. \n\nI hope you don't think I'm just being avoidant or cowardly. I really am trying my best to stay active. Is it alright that I've been skipping those things? I just thought it might be better for everyone if I stayed away for a little while until I can be more pleasant company.\n\nDoctor: How would you rate the intensity of the guilt you feel about your friend's death, on a scale from 1 to 10?\n\nPatient: Oh, a number... um, well, I don't want to sound dramatic, Doctor. I know everyone expects me to be moving on by now, and I really, really try to put on a brave face so I don't burden anyone. \n\nBut if I'm being perfectly honest with you... the guilt, along with just this constant fear and... and that anger I mentioned... it's just always there. It hasn't eased up at all, not the way people told me it would. It's just this mix of awful feelings I've been living with every single day since she was killed. \n\nSo, I suppose... maybe an 8? Or a 9? I hope that doesn't sound too extreme. I just... I really thought I'd be doing better by now. Is it normal to still feel it that strongly after all this time? I just want to make sure I'm doing the right things to get better.\n\nDoctor: Do you blame yourself for your friend's death?\n\nPatient: Oh, yes... I suppose I do. I know I touched on this just a few moments ago, and I'm so sorry for repeating myself, Doctor. I really don't want to sound like a broken record or waste our time together... but yes. \n\nI just keep telling myself that if I had only picked a different route for us, or if I had just pushed harder about waiting at that crossing, she might still be alive today. It's a dreadful thing to carry around, and I feel so ashamed that I can't just let it go. And then... well, then I get furious all over again at the driver, and at the town for letting that intersection stay so dangerous and letting this happen. \n\nI know it’s terribly ungracious of me to hold onto so much blame and anger, and I really do try to stop myself when I start pointing fingers like that. It's just so hard to control when the thoughts pop up. I hope you can understand... does that make sense? I just want to be sure I'm giving you the answers you need.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt emotionally numb or detached from others since your friend's death?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I really do try to stay connected, Doctor. I make it a point to go to the senior center and sit with the same ladies I've known for years. I don't want them to think I'm ignoring them or being rude. \n\nBut... if I'm being completely honest, and I hope this doesn't make me sound cold or unfeeling... I just don't feel the same way around them anymore. Even when I'm right there at the table, listening to them talk, I feel sort of... separated from it all. It's almost as if they're chatting on one side of a window, and I'm just watching from the other side. \n\nI feel so terrible about it because they've been so kind to me, and I really should be more engaged. I just can't seem to bridge that gap right now. Is it awful of me to feel so disconnected from my friends? I really am trying to be present, I promise.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe you're a bad person because of what happened to your friend?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... a bad person? I... I wouldn't want to use that word exactly, Doctor. I really try to be a good person, I always have. I volunteer, I help out when I can... I don't want you to think I'm some terrible, malicious woman. \n\nBut... well, I know I mentioned this earlier, and I'm sorry to bring it up again, but it's just so hard to escape. I do keep telling myself that if I had just picked a different route for our walk, or if I had pushed harder about waiting at that awful crossing... she might still be here. And when I think about that, I do feel like I failed her. It makes me feel... well, very small and ashamed. \n\nAnd then, almost immediately, I just get so furious all over again at the driver, and at the town for letting that intersection stay so dangerous and letting this happen. I know I shouldn't be so angry, and I really try to keep those uncharitable thoughts in check, because I know it doesn't do any good to be so bitter. \n\nI hope that doesn't make me sound like a bad person to you? I'm really trying to work through these feelings properly, I promise. Am I making sense? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right picture of what's going on in my head.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in activities that you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I really do try to keep busy, Doctor. I don't want to be a bother to anyone or just sit around the house moping, so I still make sure to write everything down on my calendar and keep my schedule. \n\nBut... oh, it's a bit embarrassing to admit, if I'm being perfectly honest. I was always so involved and energetic about it all. \n\nLately, though... when I look at those things on the calendar, I just... I feel almost nothing. It’s the strangest thing. Well, um... I really do try to keep up with my responsibilities, Doctor. I know people rely on me, and I've always prided myself on being dependable. I don't want you to think I've just completely abandoned everyone. 

But... oh, it's so hard to admit this... I have had to step back from some things. I used to organize the programs at the senior center, and I managed the budgets for our volunteer group. I was always so good with the numbers and keeping everything on track. Lately, though, I just... I can't seem to keep up with it reliably. My mind just isn't as sharp, or maybe I just don't have the energy. So I had to pass those duties on to someone else. 

And, well, I guess I have pulled away from the clubs and activities that used to structure my week, too. I just couldn't bring myself to go and pretend everything was normal. 

I feel awful about letting people down, I really do. I hope you understand that I didn't do it lightly. I just didn't want to make mistakes with the budget or ruin the programs for everyone else. Do you think it was okay for me to step back like that? I just want to make sure I'm handling this the right way.

I'll take a much longer route just so I don't have to walk past it. \n\nAnd I've actually stopped signing up for most of the walking tours, and even the local safety meetings. I feel so guilty about missing them, I really do, but I just... I cannot bear the conversations when they inevitably circle back to what happened there. I don't want to make a scene or make anyone else uncomfortable if I get upset, you know? Does that make sense? I hope it's okay that I've been staying away from those things.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any moments where you felt disconnected from yourself or the world around you since your friend's death?\n\nPatient: Well, um... disconnected? I'm not entirely sure I know what you mean by that, Doctor. \n\nI don't think I've ever felt like I wasn't... myself, or anything strange like that. I mean, sometimes I get a little distracted, or maybe a bit overwhelmed when there's too much going on, but I think that's just normal for someone my age, isn't it? I certainly haven't lost touch with reality or anything. \n\nI try very hard to stay present and keep up with everything. Is it... is it bad if I don't quite understand what you're looking for? I want to make sure I'm answering your questions properly. I hope I'm not being difficult.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any recurring thoughts or memories about your friend's death that you can't seem to get out of your head?\n\nPatient: Well, um... yes, I'm afraid I do. I really try not to let my mind dwell on it, I promise I do, because I know I should be focusing on the positive things. But sometimes it just... happens. \n\nIt doesn't even take much. Just hearing the chirp of a crosswalk signal, or... or the loud growl of a big truck going by. Even just seeing one of those walking-tour maps we used to use. It's like my brain just snaps right back to that day, and it's so sharp, it's almost like I'm right there again. I can see her stepping off the curb, and I can't stop it from playing out in my head. \n\nI'm so sorry, I know I shouldn't let these little things bother me so much. I feel like I ought to have better control over my own thoughts by now. Is it... is it terribly weak of me to still get so rattled by a crosswalk signal? I hope you don't think I'm just feeling sorry for myself.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble sleeping at night?\n\nPatient: Well, um... my sleep is mostly fine, I think. I mean, I do wake up sometimes, but isn't that just what happens as we get older? \n\nI really try to make sure I get enough rest so I'm not a bother to anyone the next day. Sometimes it takes a little while to drift off, maybe because I'm just thinking about what I need to do around the house, but I wouldn't call it trouble sleeping. I certainly don't want to complain about something as silly as tossing and turning a bit. \n\nI hope that's the kind of answer you were looking for? I just don't want to make a fuss over a few restless nights if it's perfectly normal for someone my age.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel on edge or easily startled?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I wouldn't say I'm exactly on edge, Doctor. I really do try to stay calm and not be a nervous Nellie about things. But, I suppose... well, whenever I am near traffic now, I just feel like I have to pay very close attention. \n\nI find myself watching every single truck, every curb, every pedestrian... almost like it's my responsibility to catch the danger before it can happen again. \n\nI know I can't control everything, and I really shouldn't be so silly about it. I just want to make sure everyone is safe, you know? I hope I'm not overreacting by doing that. Is that... is that the sort of thing you mean? I just think it pays to be a little extra careful, but I certainly don't want to sound like I'm letting my nerves get the better of me.\n\nDoctor: How often do you think about the guilt you feel related to your friend's death?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I really try not to think about it too much, Doctor. I know it's not helpful to dwell on the past, and I certainly don't want to seem like I'm just wallowing in pity. \n\nBut... well, I suppose it does cross my mind more often than I'd like to admit. I just... I keep telling myself that if I had only picked a different route for our walk, or if I had just pushed harder about waiting at that crossing... maybe she might still be here. \n\nAnd then, oh dear, I shouldn't say this, but I get so terribly furious all over again at the driver, and even the town for letting that intersection be so dangerous. I know I shouldn't be so angry, and I really try to be forgiving. It's just... it's hard sometimes. \n\nI hope you don't think poorly of me for having such uncharitable thoughts. I know I should be more accepting of what happened. Is it awful of me to still feel so upset about it?\n\nDoctor: How often would you say you think about what you could have done differently to prevent your friend's death?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I suppose I did just sort of mention that, didn't I? I hope I'm not repeating myself too much, Doctor, I certainly don't want to waste your time. \n\nBut to answer your question about how often... I suppose it's almost every single day, if I'm being completely honest. I really do try to busy myself with chores so I don't dwell on it, because I know it doesn't do anyone any good. But the thoughts just sneak up on me. \n\nI just keep replaying it, telling myself that if I had only chosen a different street for our walk, or if I had just grabbed her arm and made us wait at that crossing... she'd still be alive. And then... well, like I said, I get so horribly angry at that driver all over again, and at the town for ignoring how dangerous that intersection is. \n\nI know it's entirely unhelpful to keep pointing fingers, especially at myself, and I really am trying to be better about it. I know I shouldn't let my mind go there so often and get so worked up. Am I making sense? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Have you avoided any other activities or places beyond the intersection and walking tours since your friend's death?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I really do try to keep up with my usual routine, Doctor. I don't want to be a bother to anyone or seem like I'm hiding away. But, I suppose... well, I do go quite a bit out of my way to avoid that particular intersection now. I just find it easier to take a different route, even if it's longer. \n\nAnd, um... I actually haven't been signing up for most of the walking tours lately, or even the neighborhood safety meetings. I know I should stay involved, and I feel terribly guilty for stepping back. It's just... I really can't bear it when the conversations circle back to what happened there. It just brings up all those unpleasant feelings again, and I don't want to make anyone uncomfortable by getting upset. \n\nI hope you don't think I'm just being avoidant or cowardly. I really am trying my best to stay active. Is it alright that I've been skipping those things? I just thought it might be better for everyone if I stayed away for a little while until I can be more pleasant company.\n\nDoctor: How would you rate the intensity of the guilt you feel about your friend's death, on a scale from 1 to 10?\n\nPatient: Oh, a number... um, well, I don't want to sound dramatic, Doctor. I know everyone expects me to be moving on by now, and I really, really try to put on a brave face so I don't burden anyone. \n\nBut if I'm being perfectly honest with you... the guilt, along with just this constant fear and... and that anger I mentioned... it's just always there. It hasn't eased up at all, not the way people told me it would. It's just this mix of awful feelings I've been living with every single day since she was killed. \n\nSo, I suppose... maybe an 8? Or a 9? I hope that doesn't sound too extreme. I just... I really thought I'd be doing better by now. Is it normal to still feel it that strongly after all this time? I just want to make sure I'm doing the right things to get better.\n\nDoctor: Do you blame yourself for your friend's death?\n\nPatient: Oh, yes... I suppose I do. I know I touched on this just a few moments ago, and I'm so sorry for repeating myself, Doctor. I really don't want to sound like a broken record or waste our time together... but yes. \n\nI just keep telling myself that if I had only picked a different route for us, or if I had just pushed harder about waiting at that crossing, she might still be alive today. It's a dreadful thing to carry around, and I feel so ashamed that I can't just let it go. And then... well, then I get furious all over again at the driver, and at the town for letting that intersection stay so dangerous and letting this happen. \n\nI know it’s terribly ungracious of me to hold onto so much blame and anger, and I really do try to stop myself when I start pointing fingers like that. It's just so hard to control when the thoughts pop up. I hope you can understand... does that make sense? I just want to be sure I'm giving you the answers you need.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt emotionally numb or detached from others since your friend's death?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I really do try to stay connected, Doctor. I make it a point to go to the senior center and sit with the same ladies I've known for years. I don't want them to think I'm ignoring them or being rude. \n\nBut... if I'm being completely honest, and I hope this doesn't make me sound cold or unfeeling... I just don't feel the same way around them anymore. Even when I'm right there at the table, listening to them talk, I feel sort of... separated from it all. It's almost as if they're chatting on one side of a window, and I'm just watching from the other side. \n\nI feel so terrible about it because they've been so kind to me, and I really should be more engaged. I just can't seem to bridge that gap right now. Is it awful of me to feel so disconnected from my friends? I really am trying to be present, I promise.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe you're a bad person because of what happened to your friend?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... a bad person? I... I wouldn't want to use that word exactly, Doctor. I really try to be a good person, I always have. I volunteer, I help out when I can... I don't want you to think I'm some terrible, malicious woman. \n\nBut... well, I know I mentioned this earlier, and I'm sorry to bring it up again, but it's just so hard to escape. I do keep telling myself that if I had just picked a different route for our walk, or if I had pushed harder about waiting at that awful crossing... she might still be here. And when I think about that, I do feel like I failed her. It makes me feel... well, very small and ashamed. \n\nAnd then, almost immediately, I just get so furious all over again at the driver, and at the town for letting that intersection stay so dangerous and letting this happen. I know I shouldn't be so angry, and I really try to keep those uncharitable thoughts in check, because I know it doesn't do any good to be so bitter. \n\nI hope that doesn't make me sound like a bad person to you? I'm really trying to work through these feelings properly, I promise. Am I making sense? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right picture of what's going on in my head.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in activities that you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I really do try to keep busy, Doctor. I don't want to be a bother to anyone or just sit around the house moping, so I still make sure to write everything down on my calendar and keep my schedule. \n\nBut... oh, it's a bit embarrassing to admit, if I'm being perfectly honest. I used to just love planning out my garden beds for the spring, and quilting with the ladies. We'd go to theater outings, and I used to argue so happily at our book club. I was always so involved and energetic about it all. \n\nLately, though... when I look at those things on the calendar, I just... I feel almost nothing. It’s the strangest thing. It almost feels like those hobbies belong to someone I used to be, rather than who I am now. \n\nI feel so terribly guilty saying that out loud, because I know I should be grateful to have such lovely hobbies and things to do. I hope you don't think I'm just giving up or being lazy. I really am trying to push through it. Does that make sense, what I'm trying to say? I just want to be sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to find meaningful or enjoyable that you have completely stopped doing since your friend's death?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I really do try to keep up with my responsibilities, Doctor. I know people rely on me, and I've always prided myself on being dependable. I don't want you to think I've just completely abandoned everyone. \n\nBut... oh, it's so hard to admit this... I have had to step back from some things. I used to organize the programs at the senior center, and I managed the budgets for our volunteer group. I was always so good with the numbers and keeping everything on track. Lately, though, I just... I can't seem to keep up with it reliably. My mind just isn't as sharp, or maybe I just don't have the energy. So I had to pass those duties on to someone else. \n\nAnd, well, I guess I have pulled away from the clubs and activities that used to structure my week, too. I just couldn't bring myself to go and pretend everything was normal. \n\nI feel awful about letting people down, I really do. I hope you understand that I didn't do it lightly. I just didn't want to make mistakes with the budget or ruin the programs for everyone else. Do you think it was okay for me to step back like that? I just want to make sure I'm handling this the right way.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, um... well, I really try to be strong, Doctor. I know everyone expects me to handle things gracefully, and I don't want to be someone who just falls apart or wallows in sadness. I always try to put on a brave face for the people around me. 

But... if I'm being completely honest, and I hope you don't think less of me for this... I do find myself going out of my way to avoid certain things. Like... well, I absolutely will not drive or walk near that intersection anymore. I just take a completely different route, even if it adds twenty minutes to my trip. And I've stopped signing up for the neighborhood tours, or those safety meetings they hold at the community center. 

I know it probably seems a bit silly, or maybe even cowardly, to just run away from it all. But I just cannot bear the conversations that always seem to circle back to what happened there. Hearing people talk about it, or even just seeing the spot... it brings up feelings that are just too overwhelming, and I don't want to break down in public. 

I feel terrible admitting that I'm avoiding things, because I know I should be facing my problems head-on. I hope you don't think I'm just being weak. Is it normal to want to stay away from things like that? I just want to make sure I'm not doing something completely wrong by trying to protect myself a little bit.

It's like my brain just snaps right back to that day, and it's so sharp, it's almost like I'm right there again. I can see her stepping off the curb, and I can't stop it from playing out in my head. \n\nI'm so sorry, I know I shouldn't let these little things bother me so much. I feel like I ought to have better control over my own thoughts by now. Is it... is it terribly weak of me to still get so rattled by a crosswalk signal? I really try not to think about it too much, Doctor. I know I shouldn't be so angry, and I really try to be forgiving. It's just... it's hard sometimes. \n\nI hope you don't think poorly of me for having such uncharitable thoughts. Is it awful of me to still feel so upset about it?\n\nDoctor: How often would you say you think about what you could have done differently to prevent your friend's death?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I suppose I did just sort of mention that, didn't I? I hope I'm not repeating myself too much, Doctor, I certainly don't want to waste your time. \n\nBut to answer your question about how often... I suppose it's almost every single day, if I'm being completely honest. I really do try to busy myself with chores so I don't dwell on it, because I know it doesn't do anyone any good. And then... well, like I said, I get so horribly angry at that driver all over again, and at the town for ignoring how dangerous that intersection is. \n\nI know it's entirely unhelpful to keep pointing fingers, especially at myself, and I really am trying to be better about it. I know I shouldn't let my mind go there so often and get so worked up. Am I making sense? I actually haven't been signing up for most of the walking tours lately, or even the neighborhood safety meetings. I know I should stay involved, and I feel terribly guilty for stepping back. It's just... I really can't bear it when the conversations circle back to what happened there. It just brings up all those unpleasant feelings again, and I don't want to make anyone uncomfortable by getting upset. \n\nI hope you don't think I'm just being avoidant or cowardly. I really am trying my best to stay active. Is it alright that I've been skipping those things? I just thought it might be better for everyone if I stayed away for a little while until I can be more pleasant company.\n\nDoctor: How would you rate the intensity of the guilt you feel about your friend's death, on a scale from 1 to 10?\n\nPatient: Oh, a number... um, well, I don't want to sound dramatic, Doctor. I used to organize the programs at the senior center, and I managed the budgets for our volunteer group. I was always so good with the numbers and keeping everything on track. Lately, though, I just... I can't seem to keep up with it reliably. My mind just isn't as sharp, or maybe I just don't have the energy. So I had to pass those duties on to someone else. \n\nAnd, well, I guess I have pulled away from the clubs and activities that used to structure my week, too. I just couldn't bring myself to go and pretend everything was normal. \n\nI feel awful about letting people down, I really do. I hope you understand that I didn't do it lightly. I just didn't want to make mistakes with the budget or ruin the programs for everyone else. Do you think it was okay for me to step back like that? I just want to make sure I'm handling this the right way.\n\nDoctor: Do you try to avoid feeling certain emotions related to your friend's death?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I really try to be strong, Doctor. Well, um... I really do try to stay present, Doctor. I know it's important to pay attention to what's happening around me, especially at my age, and I don't want you to think I'm just wandering around in a daze or losing my faculties. I always try to stay focused.

When that happens, it's like... well, it suddenly puts the whole scene right back in my head, so sharply. It's so vivid that I can actually see my friend stepping off the curb, right there in front of me, before I can even stop the thought. And for a moment, I suppose I do lose track of where I actually am, because it just feels so real again.

I feel so foolish when it happens, like I should have better control over my own mind. I hope you don't think I'm going crazy or anything like that. I really am trying to keep those thoughts away. Is it common for things to just pop into your head like that, even when you're trying so hard not to let them? I just want to be sure I'm explaining it properly.
